Given a single image, OmniRay predicts a ray direction and an up-to-scale radial distance for every pixel, from which a 3D point cloud is recovered without needing camera information at inference time. Extensive experiments across different camera configurations demonstrate that OmniRay establishes a new state-of-the-art in camera-agnostic monocular 3D reconstruction.
  </details>

- **[GigaBench: Benchmarking Learnable Representations for Gigapixel Image Compression and Super-Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31644)**  `arXiv:2610.31644`  
  _Matteo Fusconi, Alex Costanzino, Pierluigi Zama Ramirez, Luigi Di Stefano_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Learnable Representations (LRs) can fit parametric families of functions to diverse kinds of signals. These emergent neural-native data formats are gathering interest in the scientific community due to their compactness, resolution-agnosticism and amenability to direct neural processing. On the other hand, gigapixel images are becoming increasingly widespread across a variety of scientific, cultural, and industrial domains. In this paper, we present GigaBench, the first benchmark and experimental evaluation focused on deploying LRs with gigapixel imagery. GigaBench includes images collected, acquired, and synthetically rendered across diverse domains, and addresses image compression and super-resolution. We adapt a representative set of LRs, spanning Implicit, Hybrid, and Explicit methods, so as to operate reliably at the gigapixel scale on consumer hardware. Through systematic evaluation across reconstruction quality, model size, compression and inference times, we identify consistent trends across methods and suggest actionable guidance for future investigation.
  </details>

- **[UniVOE: A Removal-Driven Framework for Interaction-Aware Universal Video Object Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31641)**  `arXiv:2610.31641`  
  _Shuzhou Yang, Lanqing Guo, Lingen Li, Weiqi Li, Zhao Zhong, Liefeng Bo, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video object removal and injection are reciprocal editing operations between object-present and object-absent video states, yet they differ substantially in training difficulty. Removal can be effectively bootstrapped from video inpainting and further adapted to erase object-related interaction effects. Injection, however, requires large-scale paired supervision to model object appearance, dynamics, and scene-dependent effects, while perfectly aligned real-world pairs are difficult to collect and synthetic data lacks sufficient realism and diversity. To address this challenge, we present UniVOE, a removal-driven framework for training both video object removal and injection models. We first train an effect-aware remover by transferring video inpainting priors and finetuning on object-present/object-absent pairs. The trained remover converts real object-present videos into object-absent counterparts, creating large-scale paired supervision for injection. An injection model is trained on these pairs in the reverse direction to recover objects together with their dynamics and interaction effects. Moreover, since pixel-level reconstruction alone cannot adequately capture perceptual realism, we introduce a reward-based post-training method that aligns injection results with human preferences using a proprietary reward model. Experiments against strong open-source and commercial systems demonstrate that UniVOE achieves state-of-the-art performance on both object removal and injection, particularly in challenging interaction-effect cases.
  </details>

- **[WorldForge V2: Eliciting Camera-Trajectory Alignment in Video Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31640)**  `arXiv:2610.31640`  
  _Chenxi Song, Yanming Yang, Tong Zhao, Ruibo Li, Joey Tianyi Zhou, Chi Zhang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video diffusion models have learned rich 3D/4D priors, making them promising for scene generation and video re-rendering. However, their use in spatial tasks is limited by imprecise camera control, spatial and temporal inconsistencies, and entangled camera motion with scene dynamics. Existing approaches, such as task-specific fine-tuning and post-process warping, often introduce visual artifacts, generalize poorly, or incur high computational costs. We propose WorldForge V2 to achieve accurate camera control while preserving the generative capabilities of pretrained video models. We first develop a training-free guidance framework using warped references to iteratively refine generation along a target camera path. Optical-flow analysis identifies motion-related latent channels for selective guidance, helping separate motion control from appearance. A dual-path strategy further compares guided and unguided predictions to correct artifacts from imperfect references. Together, these components guide viewpoint changes while preserving scene appearance and object motion. To reduce refinement costs, v-cache approximates the correction using the preceding denoising prediction, avoiding an additional model evaluation. We further extend motion localization from latent channels to network blocks and introduce Trajectory LoRA, which concentrates adaptation on blocks most responsive to trajectory guidance. A two-stage training strategy teaches these adapters to denoise the states encountered during guided generation, using limited supervision effectively. This enables reliable camera control with very little training data, with a single training scene already supporting generalization to unseen scenes. Quantitative and qualitative evaluations across multiple datasets show that our method achieves state-of-the-art generation quality and trajectory accuracy in camera-controlled video generation.
  </details>

- **[HiP3R: Hierarchical Pose Graphs for Long-Horizon 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31635)**  `arXiv:2610.31635`  
  _Zunnan Xu, Shangchen Zhou, Yihang Luo, Chen Change Loy_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Feed-forward models accurately estimate camera motion and 3D geometry from short video windows. However, computational and memory costs prevent direct inference on sequences containing thousands of frames. Long videos must therefore be processed as overlapping chunks. This division produces independent local reconstructions with inconsistent coordinate frames and duplicate estimates of shared cameras. Achieving global consistency requires propagating constraints from adjacent overlaps and distant revisits across the sequence. This requirement naturally motivates pose graph optimization. However, the choice of graph level creates a trade-off. Chunk-level graphs efficiently resolve scale drift and close large loops. Yet, their rigid transformations cannot correct internal errors or conflicting frame predictions. Conversely, frame-level optimization offers local flexibility, but scaling it globally is computationally expensive and can break metric scale. HiP3R resolves this trade-off using a hierarchical pose graph. HiP3R first optimizes a robust chunk-level $\mathrm{Sim}(3)$ graph to establish the global layout and scale. It then holds this layout fixed and estimates one shared $\mathrm{SE}(3)$ camera per frame, turning overlapping window predictions into a single trajectory. Experiments across multiple benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of HiP3R in improving camera trajectories and 3D reconstruction. Our method applies to both chunk-based and streaming backends without additional training or fine-tuning. The codes are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[F2G-Pose: Geometry-Aware Foundation Feature Lifting for Direct RGB-D Category-Level Object Pose Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31623)**  `arXiv:2610.31623`  
  _Jinyu Zhang, Haitao Lin, Jiashu Hou, Xiangyang Xue, Yanwei Fu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  This paper studies category-level RGB-D object pose estimation, which recovers an object's 3D rotation and translation without instance-specific CAD models or reference views. This task is highly ambiguous due to intra-class variations, symmetries, and partial observations. Current solutions struggle to balance reliability and efficiency: methods based on intermediate structures are prone to error propagation, while diffusion models suffer from high inference latency and complex post-hoc candidate extraction. To address this, we present F2G-Pose, a real-time direct, single-pass framework that predicts category-level pose, metric size, and camera-frame completed shape from a single RGB-D observation in one feed-forward pass. F2G-Pose lifts dense visual foundation features to partial point clouds, encodes local geometry with DGCNN tokenization, and integrates object-level structure with a Mixture-of-Experts Transformer. Joint size prediction and camera-frame shape completion encourage metric consistency through dense geometric supervision. With its task-specific network trained only on pose-annotated synthetic SOPE data and its frozen RADIO backbone externally pretrained, F2G-Pose achieves state-of-the-art accuracy on SOPE and ROPE while running at 28 FPS for pose-only inference, and further demonstrates strong camera-frame geometry on HouseCat6D and real-object transfer on HANDAL. Analyses of latency, robustness, shape completion, and GenPose++ candidate behavior further demonstrate the efficiency and reliability of F2G-Pose, with real-world manipulation experiments demonstrating deployment potential under segmented RGB-D inputs. The project page, code, and pretrained models are made available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Beyond Best-of-$k$: Prior-Consistent DAPS for Ambiguous Nonlinear Inverse Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31592)**  `arXiv:2610.31592`  
  _Merve Kocabas, Benjamin A. Burns, Andreas Geiger, Kent Gauen, Sara Fridovich-Keil_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Diffusion models have become strong priors for solving inverse problems. However, nonlinear inverse problems such as phase retrieval remain challenging when multiple measurement-consistent solutions are distinguishable only by the prior; we call such measurements ambiguous. Existing diffusion-based samplers rely on local approximations during reconstruction. For an ambiguous measurement, these approximations can allow measurement guidance to favor a measurement-consistent mode that is close to a prior-supported mode but is itself not supported by the prior. Evaluation often masks this type of failure by reporting the best-of-$k$ runs, where the best is selected by comparison to the ground truth. We introduce Prior-Consistent DAPS (PC-DAPS), a variant of Decoupled Annealing Posterior Sampling (DAPS) designed to address this failure case by maintaining multiple candidate solutions within a single sampling run. As annealing noise decreases, PC-DAPS prunes and replaces weaker candidates according to their prior consistency, without any ground-truth access. PC-DAPS reduces the incidence of prior-inconsistent samples and improves sample quality on nonlinear inverse problems while preserving sample quality and diversity on linear inverse problems.
  </details>

- **[CellTrack-Bench: Microscopic Cell Tracking Benchmark for Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31566)**  `arXiv:2610.31566`  
  _Ilan Theodoro, Jordão Bragantini, Loïc A. Royer_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Large language models have shown outstanding performance on text-first tasks such as coding, math, and writing. Recent efforts are shifting models' training to include scientific problems that often live in highly specialized domains beyond text, such as protein structure and biological images. However, in these scenarios, their general-purpose accuracy compared with specialized algorithms remains in question. One such domain is cell tracking, which requires spatiotemporal awareness of cell dynamics. To evaluate this, we created a new benchmark, CellTrack-Bench, which assesses Vision-Language Models' (VLMs) cell-tracking-related performance against specialized trackers. The full benchmark draws from 15 standard cell-tracking datasets across four imaging modalities and comprises 1,537 problems corresponding to 9,041 cell-linking decisions. We selected a hard subset requiring greater reasoning effort by identifying failures shared across distinct algorithmic trackers. With this proposed dataset, we: (i) evaluated different environment harnesses for VLMs; (ii) benchmarked 9 frontier closed-source models and 13 open-weight models; and (iii) provided a detailed analysis of their failure modes. Code, the stored model outputs, and a pipeline that regenerates the benchmark from the source datasets are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Squaring-the-Sphere: Distortion-Free and Seamless Panorama Generation via CubeAtlas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31565)**  `arXiv:2610.31565`  
  _Xuhui Zuo, Chenjie Cao, Tengfei Wang, Junta Wu, Yisu Zhang, Chunchao Guo_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Generating 360-degree panoramas requires both local perspective fidelity and global spherical continuity. Existing representations face a trade-off: equirectangular projection (ERP) enables seamless panorama modeling but suffers from severe distortion and a domain gap to pre-trained image generators, whereas cubemaps preserve perspective geometry but often produce inconsistent faces and visible seams. We present Squaring-the-Sphere, a panoramic image generation framework built on a novel representation CubeAtlas, which unfolds all cubemap faces into a single compact canvas. By arranging orientation-aligned, rotated polar regions alongside the equatorial faces, this representation preserves the perspective geometry of cubemaps while organizing the cross-face relationships required for seamless panorama assembly. It further enables a pretrained diffusion transformer to model the complete panorama jointly in a single pass, without altering the backbone architecture or positional encoding. While CubeAtlas has a token count comparable to ERP, its structured layout makes many dense attention interactions unnecessary. We therefore introduce a layout-aware sparse attention mask that preserves the interactions required for panorama continuity while removing geometrically irrelevant token pairs. As a result, CubeAtlas achieves better quality than ERP modeling with lower attention computation. Furthermore, we introduce a training-free super-resolution technique and a fusion scheme that enhance fine details in distortion-free perspective space before achieving the final ERP panorama. Experiments on various benchmarks demonstrate that our method improves geometric fidelity, cross-view consistency, and high-resolution visual quality over previous methods.
  </details>

- **[LACToS: Local Anomaly Detection via Conditional Typicality of Superpixels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31538)**  `arXiv:2610.31538`  
  _Jihoon Hong, Alireza Kheirandish, Rohan Sanda, Sara Fridovich-Keil_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Local anomaly detection is the task of identifying spatially confined anomalous or out-of-distribution (OOD) regions within an otherwise in-distribution (ID) image. Most existing methods are designed for a specific context, with a strong focus on industrial defect detection, where they achieve excellent performance on well-established benchmarks. However, we observe that these methods can exhibit significant performance drops in other contexts, especially when the inlier distribution is multimodal. To account for this inlier complexity, we propose LACToS, a principled local OOD detection method based on statistical typicality and made tractable through two innovations: (i) grouping pixels into superpixels and (ii) using a computationally efficient proxy to estimate typicality. We evaluate LACToS across three diverse applications spanning medical imaging, human faces, and X-ray security screening. LACToS performs more robustly than prior methods across these benchmarks. The code is released here:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[Diffusion-Based Synthesis of Complete 3D Biparametric Prostate MRI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31523)**  `arXiv:2610.31523`  
  _Claudia Giardina, Montse Pardàs, Verónica Vilaplana_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Synthetic biparametric MRI (bpMRI) may help alleviate the limited availability of large and representative prostate MRI datasets for deep learning applications. In this work, we present a conditional 3D diffusion framework for anatomy-conditioned synthesis of complete prostate bpMRI from anatomical masks. The framework supports sequential synthesis, in which T2w and diffusion modalities are generated by separately trained models, and joint synthesis, in which all bpMRI modalities are generated simultaneously within a single model. Five configurations were trained using PI-CAI, FastMRI Prostate, and PROSTATEx datasets to investigate different conditioning schemes, target modalities, and synthesis strategies. Evaluation using distribution-based metrics, paired-image similarity, and qualitative assessment demonstrated the feasibility of both synthesis pathways. Joint synthesis achieved competitive distributional fidelity while generating complete bpMRI, whereas direct synthesis of ADC and computed high-b-value DWI outperformed their derivation from synthetic diffusion-weighted images.
  </details>

- **[Evaluation of Uncertainty-Aware Multi-Software Ensembles for Hippocampal Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31505)**  `arXiv:2610.31505`  
  _Gabriel Oliveira-Stahl, Anna Schroder, James Moggridge, Hamza A. Salhab, Caroline Micallef, Josephine Barnes, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Accurate hippocampal segmentation can be a useful tool for diagnosing and monitoring neurological conditions such as Alzheimer's disease and epilepsy. While numerous automated segmentation methods exist, their clinical adoption remains limited. Reliable uncertainty assessment can enhance trust and facilitate clinical translation. This study evaluates ensembles of five heterogeneous hippocampal segmentation methods - InnerEye, ASHS, FastSurfer, HippoSeg, and FreeSurfer - across two dementia datasets and one epilepsy dataset. The sub-ensemble containing InnerEye, FastSurfer, and HippoSeg emerged as both accurate and efficient, highlighting the feasibility of balancing computational cost and performance. Additionally, ensemble-derived uncertainty quantification with sample variance, mutual information, and predictive entropy is shown to reduce inaccurate segmentations by flagging low-confidence cases, potentially providing a mechanism for automatically escalating ambiguous cases for expert assessment. Lastly, this study explores the downstream clinical utility of ensemble-derived uncertainty, finding a significant correlation with motion artefact severity and a modest contribution to Alzheimer's disease classification.
  </details>

- **[AgentVQA: A Unified Benchmark for Agentic Visual Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31494)**  `arXiv:2610.31494`  
  _Advait Gupta, Sachin Konan, Tom Yin, Gabriel Sarch, Zhuang Liu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-language models (VLMs) can perform a broad range of tasks across diverse settings, yet their performance in agentic contexts remains poorly understood. Although online evaluations in simulators and real-world environments are essential for measuring interactive agent performance, they are costly and challenging to scale. Complementary offline VQA benchmarks can enable faster and more reliable comparisons across agentic domains, yet existing benchmarks remain limited and fragmented. To address this gap, we introduce AgentVQA, an offline benchmark for evaluating VLMs on realistic agentic scenarios across five agent domains: Web Agents, Robotics, Egocentric Videos, Games, and Spatial Planning. We build AgentVQA by manually selecting 1000 challenging, real-world agent examples from 23 source datasets and adapting them for reliable offline evaluation. The resulting benchmark preserves realistic scenarios from interactive environments across diverse domains, and its model rankings track those from corresponding online evaluations. Across the 22 evaluated models, the best achieves only 58.5% accuracy. Our cross-domain analysis shows that errors are driven primarily by failures to ground and maintain accurate representations of the current and evolving visual state, including localizing relevant UI elements, interpreting motion, tracking state changes over time, and inferring 3D spatial relationships, rather than by task misinterpretation or downstream logical reasoning.
  </details>

- **[MoG-VLM: Efficient Video Anomaly Detection through Motion-Guided Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31478)**  `arXiv:2610.31478`  
  _Khaled Waleed Dawoud, Muhammad Zaigham Zaheer, Khan Muhammad, Muhammad Haris Khan_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have shown strong potential for video anomaly detection, but dense frame-level inference makes them impractical for real-time deployment. Existing acceleration commonly relies on uniform sampling, which ignores content dynamics and can either waste computation on static segments or miss localized anomalies. We propose MoG-VLM a motion-adaptive anomaly detection framework that leverages motion purely as a lightweight control signal to decide when VLM inference is necessary. To this end, we introduce Temporal Gradient Magnitude (TGM) which performs content-aware sampling to concentrate computation on informative regions while avoiding redundant processing. We further introduce a hierarchical dual-stream design in which dense multi-frame VLM reasoning is activated only around frames with elevated anomaly likelihood, via anchor-triggered context-window expansion with early termination. A fusion module then reconciles these instantaneous semantic cues with temporal action dynamics. Across four benchmarks, our method achieves 91.91%, 84.30%, 87.88%, and 78.96% AUC-ROC on XD-Violence, UCF-Crime, MSAD, and ShanghaiTech, respectively, establishing new state of the art on three benchmarks under the zero-shot, training-free setting. On XD-Violence, we improve over the previous best result by +0.57% while delivering a 1.31x inference speedup. These results demonstrate that content-aware allocation of VLM capacity, processing fewer frames more intelligently rather than all frames uniformly, is a more effective axis of progress than uniformly densifying VLM inference across frames. Code is available on GitHub:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[Ask About the Right Moment: Training-Free Object Identity Reasoning for Video-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31471)**  `arXiv:2610.31471`  
  _Di Yang, Xiaochang Li, Muyang Liu, Yufei Wang, Yanhai Xiong_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Many questions about a video concern one physical object. Where does the ball end up after the cups are shuffled? Which card is the queen? How often was the mug covered? Video-language models (Video-LMs) recognise such objects in single frames but do not keep them apart over time. On real shell-game videos, open models from 8B to 235B parameters, their thinking variants, a reinforcement-learned tool agent and three training-free tool agents all stay within ten points of chance. We separate semantic identity binding from temporal identity maintenance. An object's identity should be fixed at a moment when the evidence for it is visible, and carried afterwards by a tracker. \emph{Track, Then Bind} implements this principle without training. A promptable video tracker follows every candidate object and draws it with a track number. A frozen vision-language model answers a static question about the identifying evidence, which binds the target to a track. The answer is then derived from the tracked state in text, without asking the model to reinterpret the original video during read-out. A single configuration covers all benchmarks. With Qwen3-VL-32B it solves both VET-Bench tasks, where a fine-tuned trajectory model reaches 92\% and 90\% and the same VLM voting over 16 samples stays at 30--32\%. It answers 55.4\% of the hardest Perception Test cup games, where the best published model answers 41.5\%, and, using track-derived timelines, 48.6\% of TOC-Bench questions, against 37.1\% for the same model answering directly. Drawing the same tracks on the video and asking the question directly falls 12--19 points short of the agent on real cup games.
  </details>

- **[AnyGameRenderer: Entity-Consistent Generative Rendering Across Games and an Entity-Level Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31466)**  `arXiv:2610.31466`  
  _Ruicong Liu, Liangyang Ouyang, Fengbo Lan, Zhixiang Wang, Kaipeng Zhang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  AI coding agents can now build small playable games in minutes, but the results usually look like programmer art: gray blockouts, colored geometric primitives, and flat semantic blobs. We present AnyGameRenderer, a video-to-video generative renderer that turns such footage into high-quality visuals with characters, motion detail, and effects. Throughout, it keeps the layout, camera, and every entity's position and timing intact.Our AnyGameRenderer generalizes across a wide range of genres that agents build, such as tower defense, auto-battlers, top-down survivors, turn-based battles, kart racing, and crowd runners.AnyGameRenderer takes plain video as its game-state input, with no complex representations such as engine G-buffers, per-object class labels, or low-poly proxies. Gray blockouts, colored primitives, semantic maps, and wireframes all work as input.It also preserves entity correspondence: group counts stay correct, and entities appear and disappear on the right frame.We implement AnyGameRenderer by LoRA fine-tuning MiniMax-H3 ref2va, a 33B reference-video-conditioned video generation model. Training uses $\sim$10k paired clips from three sources: (i) multi-stream rendering data from 38 agent-made games exported from GEAR, an AI game-creation tool; (ii) two-stream rendering data from Unreal Engine, with programmatically controlled crowds, count changes, and entity scales; and (iii) real gameplay recordings, with pairs reconstructed via 3D-box detection.To evaluate the entity correspondence, we propose an entity-level benchmark of 59 held-out cases with 753 entities, scored against ground-truth boxes. Compared with the base model, AnyGameRenderer better preserves entity correspondence: temporal match rises from 50.9\% to 62.9\%, missed count changes fall from 34.7\% to 21.3\%, and fewer entities are hallucinated or left unrendered.
  </details>

- **[MixPT: Point Transformer with Interleaved Window and Global Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31455)**  `arXiv:2610.31455`  
  _Minkyun Seo, Hyungtae Lim, Jaesik Park_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Point cloud transformers aim to capture detailed local geometry and relationships across a scene at a manageable computational cost. Two common approaches are window attention and global attention. They differ in how they enable scene-wide interaction, which refers to information exchange among tokens across a scene. Window attention reduces computational cost by restricting self-attention to groups of tokens called windows; in serialized designs, each window contains a fixed number of consecutive tokens from a spatially ordered sequence. Because each block only directly connects tokens within the same window, information exchange across windows relies on changes in window membership across blocks and hierarchical pooling. Global attention, in contrast, directly connects all tokens within a scene, but computing attention over all token pairs can be costly when the number of tokens is large. To balance computational cost and scene-wide interaction, we introduce MixPT, a point transformer that uses a mix of window and global attention blocks in an interleaved schedule, while retaining a single fixed serialization pattern for its window blocks. For scenes with a large number of tokens, MixPT applies global attention to spatially pooled tokens and returns residual updates to the original-resolution stream. Across indoor and outdoor benchmarks, MixPT achieves competitive accuracy with lower latency than representative window and global attention baselines. We further demonstrate the feasibility of scaling the attention stage in depth by repeating the interleaved pattern of window and global attention blocks.
  </details>

- **[WeVisOne-VL: Unified Visual Perception from Objects to Pixels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31329)**  `arXiv:2610.31329`  
  _Tianming Liang, Junyi Pan, Yuxi Li, Canmiao Fu, Zhipeng Huang, Chong Sun, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing vision-language models (VLMs) remain unreliable for fine-grained visual perception. In this report, we present WeVisOne-VL (WeChat Vision All-in-One), a powerful VLM that unifies object-level and pixel-level perception through structured next-token prediction. It supports multiple visual tasks including object detection, referring localization, counting, OCR, segmentation, and matting, enabling localization at any user-specific granularity, from points and boxes to masks and alpha mattes. Architecturally, we integrate a lightweight pixel decoder into the VLM via a special <OBJ> token to generate instance-level pixel outputs. We train the model with a three-stage curriculum SFT followed by mixed-task RL. Across over 30 benchmarks, WeVisOne-VL demonstrates leading performance on both object-level and pixel-level tasks, while maintaining robust rejection for object-absent queries. By releasing WeVisOne-VL to the open-source community, we hope to advance VLMs from broad visual understanding toward truly reliable perception systems for real-world deployment.
  </details>

- **[MVLoMa: Multi-View Local Feature Matching with Any Geometry Foundation Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31321)**  `arXiv:2610.31321`  
  _Xinyang Lu, Shengzhe Wei, Sheng Tang, Weiqi Zhang, Juntong Fang, Zesong Li_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Local feature matching is typically formulated for image pairs, although structure-from-motion and visual localization commonly match one reference image against many retrieved or covisible neighbours. Applying a pairwise matcher independently to these centre-neighbour edges duplicates reference-side computation and prevents the matcher from exploiting complementary multi-view geometric evidence from the remaining observations. To address these limitations, we introduce MVLoMa, a sparse star-graph matcher that extends LoMa from image pairs to multiple views. MVLoMa first uses a frozen multi-view geometry model to jointly describe all images in a star, then injects the resulting cross-view geometric context into local descriptors. It then matches the centre image to all neighbours in a single forward pass through per-view self-attention and bidirectional centre-neighbour cross-attention. For supervision, we derive positive correspondences only from depth-valid, mutually visible dense warps and explicitly penalize high-confidence matches that violate epipolar geometry. On 25-view star graphs from HyperSim, ScanNet++, Mip-NeRF 360, and Tanks and Temples, MVLoMa obtains the best macro-average relative-pose AUC among the compared methods at all three thresholds (58.65/85.41/90.99). Its matcher is 2.20 times faster than sequential evaluation of the 24 centre-neighbour edges. The same design supports four tested frozen geometry encoders -- DA3, $\pi^3$, VGGT, and VGGT-$\Omega$ -- with DA3-Giant performing best on all reported encoder-ablation metrics. Controlled ablations further isolate the effects of geometry-aware descriptors, visibility filtering, geometric negative supervision, and joint multi-view inference.
  </details>

- **[Stream4D: Streaming 4D Reconstruction with Training-free Motion Decoupling via Causal KV-Cache Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31320)**  `arXiv:2610.31320`  
  _Wei Zhang, Ying Feng, Mengtong Wu, Shiqiang Gong, Huaqi Zhang, Qi Zhang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  4D scene reconstruction requires robust handling of dynamic objects, yet existing streaming methods commonly assume static scenes, leading to pose drift and ghosting artifacts over long sequences. We introduce Stream4D, a training-free, motion-decoupled streaming reconstruction framework that can be directly integrated into causal Transformer backbones (e.g., StreamVGGT, Stream3R). Our key observation is that causal attention implicitly encodes rich temporal motion cues within the KV cache. We adaptively aggregate these cues from multiple attention layers and across temporal windows, and fuse them with depth priors to handle challenging cases such as occlusions and textureless regions, producing precise dynamic masks that decouple static and dynamic regions. We then apply a soft key masking scheme that leverages these dynamic masks to suppress interference from dynamic regions in causal attention, thereby improving camera pose estimation accuracy. Stream4D inherits the efficiency of causal attention and remains compatible with existing streaming architectures. On diverse dynamic-scene benchmarks, Stream4D significantly improves camera pose estimation and static-scene reconstruction quality, yields reliable dynamic masks, and effectively mitigates long-sequence ghosting.
  </details>

- **[Data-Driven Vision-Language Models for Remote Sensing: A Survey](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31304)**  `arXiv:2610.31304`  
  _Yue Zhou, Shujun Zhao, Xue Yang, Ruigang Li, Tianwen Zhang, Mengcheng Lan, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  During the deep learning era, innovations in remote sensing (RS) vision models primarily focused on optimizing network architectures for specific tasks and conducting end-to-end training. However, with the advent of the Vision-Language Models (VLMs) era, constrained by substantial computational resource demands, the mainstream research paradigm has shifted towards adapting general-purpose VLMs using RS vertical domain data for fine-tuning, rather than modifying their core architectures. This transition marks a change in the innovation pathway from model-centric to data-centric. Despite the increasing importance of data, the academic community still lacks a systematic, data-centric survey to guide researchers in constructing high-quality RS vision-language datasets for various application scenarios and in conducting effective model fine-tuning and performance evaluation based on these datasets. To address this gap, this paper presents a systematic review of the development status of RS datasets tailored for VLMs. It further provides a comprehensive practical guide for RS researchers in the large model era, covering the entire pipeline from data construction to fine-tuning and evaluation. This paper aims to encourage the academic community to build more diverse and higher-quality vision-language datasets for specialized RS sub-fields. Ultimately, the goal is to converge these efforts into a comprehensive RS vision-language corpus that offers extensive task coverage and a wide geographical distribution, thereby laying a solid data foundation for advancing towards general artificial intelligence in RS.
  </details>

- **[PiHand: Latent Hand-Object Interaction Priors for Physics-Based Policy Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31299)**  `arXiv:2610.31299`  
  _Niklas Berndt, Xianghui Xie, Enes Duran, Yixiao Wang, Gerard Pons-Moll_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Synthesizing physically grounded dexterous hand-object interactions is important for VR/AR, gaming, and robotics, yet remains challenging because subtle contact dynamics, diverse object geometries, and target trajectories must be jointly respected while maintaining natural grasps. Existing methods either track mocap trajectories with limited transfer to novel objects, or rely on hand-only generative priors that fail to capture coupled hand-object motion, sacrificing contact fidelity and sample efficiency. To tackle these challenges, we introduce PiHand, a Physically grounded Interaction-aware Hand motion synthesis framework. PiHand first trains expert policies that track mocap demonstrations in simulation, distills them into a unified hand-object interaction prior using a goal-conditioned VAE, and finally solve diverse dexterous manipulation tasks through bounded residual exploration in its latent space. With only a single reward and no complex curriculum training, this yields general tabletop manipulation policies that generalize to unseen objects and trajectories on GRAB and DexYCB. The same prior transfers to object catching and bimanual handover, two tasks that are rarely seen in its training data. Across all tasks, PiHand consistently outperforms baselines in all metrics. We will release the processed data and model checkpoints.
  </details>

- **[RecallGS: Geometry-Grounded Recall for Online Semantic Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31293)**  `arXiv:2610.31293`  
  _Thanh-Khoi Nguyen, Thien-Phuc Tran, Minh-Triet Tran_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Online semantic 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables open-vocabulary scene understanding from streaming observations. However, existing methods recover geometric context missing from image-derived semantic features using an additionally learned 3D network, incurring extra training and runtime overhead. We observe that such geometric-semantic evidence may already exist in the agent's observation history and can be retrieved rather than learned. Based on this insight, we introduce RecallGS (Geometry-Grounded Recall for Online Semantic Gaussian Splatting), a lightweight, training-free mechanism that treats past observations as a non-parametric geometric-semantic prior. RecallGS follows a recall-ground-fuse pipeline: CLIP retrieves relevant observations, alpha-compositing weights identify geometrically supported views, and aggregate their semantic features over each Gaussian's rendered footprint. On ScanNet and ScanNet++, RecallGS achieves strong semantic segmentation performance at 2.48 FPS, demonstrating that geometric-semantic context can be effectively recovered from observation history without an additional learned 3D branch.
  </details>

- **[Retrieval-Augmented Online Textual Memory for Episodic Memory Video Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31284)**  `arXiv:2610.31284`  
  _Raffaele Cali, Giuseppe Lando, Rosario Forte, Antonino Furnari_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Long egocentric videos pose a central challenge for multimodal language models: the visual stream must be compressed into a memory representation that can be searched efficiently while still preserving the evidence needed for question answering. Building on an online textual-memory framework in which first-person videos are converted into temporally grounded captions, this work systematically studies the role of retrieval in episodic-memory video question answering. We evaluate how different retrieval units, query formulations, context budgets, and reranking strategies affect multiple-choice answering on QAEgo4D-Closed, isolating retrieval as the main variable while keeping the underlying generated memory fixed.Our best configuration, Parent, matches fine-grained caption subchunks, then provides their full parent captions to the reasoner. It achieves 56.4 % accuracy, matching strong full-memory and retrieval baselines while exposing only a small fraction of the textual memory at question time. More importantly, our analysis shows that better temporal coverage does not necessarily translate into better answers: increasing the retrieval budget improves temporal Hit@K and Recall@K, but can reduce accuracy by introducing visually plausible distractors and uncertain caption evidence. These findings suggest that retrieval-augmented egocentric memory should not be optimized only for evidence recall, but also for the reliability, specificity, and answer-discriminative value of the retrieved context. The study motivates more structured episodic memories that move beyond raw caption retrieval toward evidence-aware representations for long-form egocentric reasoning.
  </details>

- **[ReCoG: Relational Concept Graph for Training-Free Personalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31282)**  `arXiv:2610.31282`  
  _Changseon Yu, Hyeokjun Kweon_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Training-free personalization adapts a frozen vision-language model (VLM) to recognize user-defined concepts from only a few exemplars by retrieving concept entries at inference time. While recent retriever-based methods enrich each concept with descriptive evidence, they largely treat concepts in isolation, so the retrieved information can be plausible yet non-discriminative, especially when the personalized concept set contains highly confusable, fine-grained categories. We argue that personalization is inherently relational: correct recognition depends on the specific distinctions between a concept and its nearest alternatives. To this end, we propose ReCoG, a Relational Concept Graph for retrieval-augmented personalization. ReCoG represents concepts as nodes and augments them with directed edges that explicitly capture discriminative cues between concept pairs, elicited by a frozen VLM during database construction. At inference, ReCoG retrieves a shortlist and resolves ambiguity via explicit pairwise comparisons conditioned on the corresponding relational edges, selecting the concept that consistently wins against competing candidates. We also introduce FINGER benchmark to test personalized recognition on confusable concept sets. Across FINGER, ReCoG substantially outperforms prior methods, with the largest gains in the most confusable regimes. ReCoG also achieves consistently best performance on established personalization benchmarks including captioning and VQA, confirming that relational evidence is broadly beneficial beyond fine-grained settings. Project Page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[Reinforcing intra-operative tumour localisation with weak supervision from pre-operative appearances](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31253)**  `arXiv:2610.31253`  
  _Sidhart Sami, Ali Sharjeel, Sri Atluri, Miao Zhao, Caroline Roney, Eirini Marouli, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Limited tumour visibility on intra-operative images complicates surgical navigation, including liver hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) targeting on computed tomography (CT) imaging. This makes labelled datasets difficult to curate for deep learning, and transferring pre-operative delineations through registration remains challenging due to intra-operative deformation and lack of paired pre- and intra-operative data. We propose to transfer tumour appearances learned from pre-operative magnetic resonance (MR) images to guide localisation on intra-operative CT through weak supervision. A classifier is trained to classify tumour presence on pre-operative images. This is applied to windows on intra-operative images, and despite the domain gap, it assigns higher tumour-presence probabilities on average to tumour-containing windows. These weak cross-domain signals guide a reinforcement learning agent to localise tumours on intra-operative images. We compare direct transfer, few-shot adaptation and synthetic transfer (trained using unpaired MR and CT), which translates CT into synthetic MR before localisation. On an external HCC cohort, synthetic transfer outperformed other tested methods and CT-only baselines. Our framework enables cross-modality tumour localisation without paired MR-CT training images.
  </details>

- **[Lightweight Frame Compensation for Real-Time Polyp Segmentation under Frame Skipping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31228)**  `arXiv:2610.31228`  
  _Weihao Gao_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Real-time polyp segmentation in computer-assisted colonoscopy is hampered by the difficulty of deploying high-accuracy models under strict latency budgets. Frame skipping, which performs inference every K frames and reuses or interpolates the rest, improves throughput but introduces motion blur and lesion misalignment that degrade temporal consistency. To close this gap, we propose the Lightweight Frame Compensator (LFC), a 77K-parameter plug-and-play module inserted before a frozen segmentation backbone. Operating on a three-frame window, LFC fuses consecutive frames via spatio-temporal attention into a single compensated frame, and is trained only on pseudo-labels generated by a differently seeded model, without any expert annotation. Experiments on three polyp video benchmarks show that LFC recovers most of the accuracy degraded by direct frame skipping and, in several settings, reaches the accuracy of frame-by-frame inference, while retaining a 2.05X--2.55X throughput gain. We further evaluate the temporal stability of three backbones spanning CNN, pyramid Transformer, and global-attention Transformer architectures, revealing a spectrum of sensitivity to frame skipping that arises from how locally or globally each architecture aggregates misaligned input. The compensation gain of LFC follows this spectrum: it is largest for high-capacity Transformer backbones, which achieve the best static accuracy but suffer the most under skip-frame inference. LFC thus offers a lightweight, pluggable, and deployment-friendly route to real-time polyp segmentation.
  </details>

- **[GLOW-FM: Efficient Generative Low-Light Image Enhancement with One-Step Latent Flow Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31222)**  `arXiv:2610.31222`  
  _Ariel Lapid, Yuval Haitman, Reuven Peretz, Arnon Netzer, Idit Diamant_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Low-light image enhancement (LLIE) aims to recover well-exposed and perceptually faithful images from severely degraded observations. Recent diffusion-based generative methods achieve strong perceptual quality by leveraging powerful image priors, but their iterative sampling incurs substantial computational cost, limiting practical deployment. Despite recent efficiency gains in generative LLIE, high-quality enhancement with a single function evaluation and low overall computation remains challenging, particularly under diverse real-world low-light conditions. We introduce GLOW-FM, a one-step latent flow-matching framework for efficient generative LLIE. GLOW-FM performs conditional transport in the latent space of a frozen Tiny-VAE and leverages Terminal Velocity Matching (TVM) to enable enhancement with a single function evaluation. Matched comparisons with alternative one-step objectives support TVM as an effective design choice, and, to the best of our knowledge, this is its first adaptation to image restoration and specifically to LLIE. Extensive experiments on challenging real-world benchmarks demonstrate that GLOW-FM achieves competitive image quality and state-of-the-art cross-dataset generalization while requiring only a single function evaluation and 34 GFLOPs for the small variant. These findings highlight the potential of GLOW-FM to bring generative LLIE closer to practical deployment through single-step inference and compact latent modeling. Code will be made publicly available upon publication.
  </details>

- **[Moving Regions, Not Moving Objects: Segmenting What Actually Moves under Camera Motion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31209)**  `arXiv:2610.31209`  
  _Wenhua Jin, Tiancheng Chen, Sheng Tang, Jiahao Li, Yupeng Liu, Weiqi Zhang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Moving object segmentation is conventionally formulated at the sequence and object level, marking an entire instance as moving once it moves anywhere in a video. Recent instantaneous formulations distinguish moving from stationary intervals, but still generate masks at the object level through frozen segmentation foundation models. Because the mask is derived from an instance-level proposal, motion is typically attributed to the entire instance rather than localized to the regions that actually move. Moreover, such instance-level predictions can produce masks even when no part of the instance is actually moving, revealing a more fundamental issue: motion is not an intrinsic property of an instance; it can only be determined by comparing at least two frames. Building on this premise, we introduce MoRSe, which jointly interprets two frames to predict a moving-region mask, distinguishing actual scene motion from apparent motion induced by the camera. A single RGB encoder replaces multi-stage foundation stacks, so no optical flow, point tracks, or separate mask generator is needed at inference. We release MoRSe-5K, a benchmark of 42 sequences with Static, Dynamic-Static, and Dynamic settings designed to evaluate the model's ability to distinguish and localize moving and stationary regions under different camera and scene motion conditions. MoRSe outperforms both specialized moving object segmentation methods and dense 4D reconstruction methods with motion-thresholded predictions, while producing the fewest false positives on sequences where nothing moves.
  </details>

- **[A Visual Grounding Benchmark for Retinal OCT with Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31205)**  `arXiv:2610.31205`  
  _Ronald Fecso, José Morano, Botond Fazekas, Paul Widmann-Sedlnitzky, Dorota Himel, Laetita Hinterhuber, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can answer clinical questions about medical images, but trustworthy use requires visual grounding: models should localize the pathology they report rather than rely on spurious shortcuts. Whether MLLMs can visually ground clinical findings in retinal optical coherence tomography (OCT), the most widely used imaging modality in ophthalmology, remains largely unexplored. To address this gap, we introduce RetinaGroundBench, the first benchmark for biomarker visual grounding in retinal OCT. It comprises 815 B-scans exhaustively annotated by ophthalmologists with 4,537 bounding boxes covering 13 biomarkers across 8 diseases. Because MLLMs often fragment a single lesion into multiple partial predictions, we propose AggIoU, a metric that evaluates whether the union of overlapping predicted boxes covers the annotated region. Across 27 open-source MLLMs (0.8-38B parameters), we find that visual grounding remains largely unsolved: the best general-purpose model achieves a macro F1 of just 23.7%, with consistently low recall, despite reaching up to 73% balanced accuracy on biomarker presence questions. To narrow this gap, we developed RetGrounder, an MLLM fine-tuned using an OCT-specific strategy integrating grounding and textual information. Despite its relatively small size (8B), RetGrounder improves grounding F1 to 33.9%, a 43% relative improvement. Our benchmark provides a reference point for measuring progress in the field of OCT diagnosis with MLLMs.
  </details>

- **[RaSFNet: Ray-aligned and Scan-aware Fusion Network for LiDAR Semantic Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31186)**  `arXiv:2610.31186`  
  _Chuang Chen_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  LiDAR point cloud semantic segmentation is a fundamental component of 3D perception for autonomous driving and robotics. Projection-based methods map 3D point clouds into 2D representations for feature extraction using 2D networks. However, aggregating multiple points during projection can obscure geometric differences within each frustum, while local feature extraction limits the modeling of spatial relationships across scanning regions. Moreover, proximity in the projected representation does not necessarily imply proximity in 3D space, and interactions between point and projected features may mix features from different ranges, reducing semantic discriminability. To address these challenges, we propose the Ray-aligned and Scan-aware Fusion Network (RaSFNet), which incorporates point cloud geometry and LiDAR scanning characteristics into projection-based semantic segmentation. Specifically, the Ray-Aligned Geometric Distribution Encoding (RAGDE) module characterizes point distributions within each frustum using a ray-aligned coordinate system. To model spatial context, a Scan-Topology-Aware Mamba (STAM) block organizes frustum features according to the LiDAR azimuth sampling pattern and captures long-range dependencies across scanning regions. In addition, a Range-Constrained Point-Frustum Fusion (RCPFF) mechanism uses shared range information to guide feature exchange between point and frustum representations while maintaining spatial correspondence. RaSFNet achieves mIoU scores of 70.2% and 77.7% on the SemanticKITTI and nuScenes validation sets, respectively, indicating competitive segmentation performance compared with the evaluated methods.
  </details>

- **[HiSTEVE 1.0: Harnessing Human-Interface VLA Agents for Open-World Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31162)**  `arXiv:2610.31162`  
  _Zecong Tang, Zian Guan, Yuncheng Guo, Zhanqiu Zhang, Yiwen Guo, Bo Dai_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Building virtual embodied agents that operate in open-ended games like human players is a long-standing goal requiring both long-horizon autonomy and reliable native-interface control. As a procedurally generated open world, Minecraft provides a natural testbed for studying the integration of these capabilities. Existing agents typically provide either native visuomotor control without long-horizon autonomy or high-level planning executed through environment APIs or predefined skills. To bridge this gap, we propose HiSTEVE, a hierarchical VLA agent with a capability-aware agent harness. At the orchestration level, we equip the harness with structured memory for tracking progress and a VLM planner for selecting subtasks within the VLA's capabilities. To reliably determine subtask completion, we further introduce a policy-coupled task completion state detector that conditions on VLA policy context to signal candidate completion. At the control level, a single VLA executes all subtasks with vision-only perception and native keyboard-and-mouse actions. To mitigate interference between heterogeneous GUI and non-GUI interactions, we introduce regime-specific RMSNorm branches and separate mouse-movement token embeddings. We also train the VLA through world knowledge injection, imitation learning, and reinforcement learning with environment feedback to improve reliability and recovery. HiSTEVE achieves state-of-the-art atomic-task performance, scoring 85.0% on MineStudio Bench and a 37.4% average success rate on OpenHA, while also completing long-horizon tasks such as crafting an iron pickaxe from scratch like human players.
  </details>

- **[RankAlign: Vision-Language Representation Alignment via Rank Transformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31145)**  `arXiv:2610.31145`  
  _Enzhe Zhao, Marco Fiorucci, Lamberto Ballan_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  While the Platonic Representation Hypothesis posits that vision and language models converge toward compatible latent geometries, recovering class-level correspondences between unpaired modalities without cross-modal supervision remains challenging. Existing relational matching methods compare within-modality similarity kernels directly, although the same semantic structure may be represented through different numerical parameterizations across modalities. We propose RankAlign, a rank-based normalization strategy for class-level unpaired vision-language matching. RankAlign transforms each within-modality similarity kernel into an ordinal relational representation before permutation matching, while leaving the correspondence space and matching solver unchanged. The resulting representation is invariant to strictly increasing reparameterizations of similarity values while preserving the ordinal structure used for matching. We provide exact-recovery and stability guarantees that characterize correspondence recovery under both exact and partial ordinal agreement. Experiments across diverse frozen vision and language encoders and multiple datasets demonstrate improved and more stable class-level correspondence recovery, including under label corruption and domain shift.
  </details>

- **[GM-GReFEL: Geometry-Guided Spatiotemporal State-Space Learning for Robust Dynamic Facial Expression Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31140)**  `arXiv:2610.31140`  
  _Yudhistira Arditya Pratama, Yi-Zeng Hsieh_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recognizing facial expressions in unconstrained video suffers from two coupled problems: self-attention grows quadratically in cost with clip length, and crowdsourced labels are noisy. We present GM-GReFEL, a spatiotemporal state-space network with a geometry-aware decision stage. Its Modulated GroupMamba backbone replaces a spatiotemporal Vision Transformer. Six channel groups are scanned separately, four over the frame plane and two over time, so the cost grows linearly with sequence length ($\mathcal{O}(N)$); after each layer, a Channel Affinity Modulation gate mixes the groups again. On top of the backbone, a GReFEL reliability module turns the normalized entropy of each prediction into a mixing weight between the classifier output and a vote from trainable geometric anchors, so that facial geometry counts for more when the network is unsure. The model is initialized by static-to-dynamic weight transfer and trained with a Triple Loss. In five-fold cross-validation, the best fold scores 66.57\%/76.46\% UAR/WAR on DFEW, 0.3 points or less below the strongest compared method, and 45.66\%/62.27\% on MAFW, ahead of all compared methods; on FERV39k the mean over five splits, 42.30\%/52.66\%, is competitive. The five-fold means are 64.07\%/74.54\% on DFEW, on par with recent large pre-trained models, and 38.90\%/54.37\% on MAFW, below the best of them. A paired $t$-test on DFEW indicates a modest benefit of temporal modeling (+1.61 UAR points, one-sided $p=0.031$). Attention stays on plausible facial regions, and latency is close to a ViT-B/16 baseline (47.4 vs.\ 46.8 ms per clip). Code:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[Pos4CHD: A Positive-Free Mask-Guided Counterfactual Synthesis Framework for Fetal Congenital Heart Defect Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31139)**  `arXiv:2610.31139`  
  _Wenjie Xuan, Yapeng Li, Hang Su, Chen Cheng, Juhua Liu, Sheng Zhao, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The scarcity of positive cases remains a major bottleneck in developing automated diagnostic systems for fetal Congenital Heart Defects~(CHDs), particularly for rare subtypes. Although normal cases are accessible with effort, positive cases are hard to collect due to low incidence rates and privacy concerns, causing severe class imbalance or absence of positive training samples. Existing works resort to synthesizing positives with conditional generative models, but they are task-specific and require positive training samples, hindering application for fetal CHDs. To this end, we propose a positive-free mask-guided counterfactual generation framework for fetal echocardiography, namely Pos4CHD, which enables realistic, high-quality synthesis of pseudo-positive samples from semantic masks, with only normal samples at hand. Technically, we first develop FE-Gen, a mask-guided generative model tailored for fetal heart ultrasound synthesis that mitigates histogram drift and enhances image-mask spatial alignment. Building on FE-Gen, Pos4CHD employs a mask-to-image generation paradigm for CHD synthesis. It first manipulates semantic masks to simulate fetal-heart structural anomalies and then renders anatomically consistent images. Extensive experiments on in-house datasets, together with expert evaluations, demonstrate the visual fidelity~($-12.97$ FID) and anatomical consistency~($+3.96\%$ DSC) of synthesized images. Further validations across diverse real-world CHD diagnosis tasks exhibit promising results and consistent increments, including zero-shot classification~(achieving $93.25\%$ ACC on EbA and $72.92\%$ ACC on TAPVC), lesion detection~($+6.95\%$ mAP50 on VSD), and anatomy segmentation~($+10.49\%$ DSC on fetal heart) compared with baselines. Codes and models are publicly available on \href{this https URL}{GitHub}.
  </details>

- **[WildDepth-v2: A Synchronized RGB--LiDAR--Radar Dataset and Benchmark for Metric Depth, Motion, and Tracking of Wildlife](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31123)**  `arXiv:2610.31123`  
  _Muhammad Aamir, Sangyun Shin, Naoya Muramatsu, Matthew Wijers, Amir Patel, Andrew Loveridge, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Measuring animal distance and motion in metric units underpins abundance estimation, morphometrics, and movement ecology, yet monocular video lacks metric scale and wildlife datasets lack metric ground truth. We present WildDepth 2, extending the synchronized RGB-LiDAR WildDepth dataset in three directions. (i) Imaging radar: 37 RGB-LiDAR-4D-radar sequences (19,716 frames) with per-point Doppler velocity and a measured error budget, to our knowledge the first imaging-radar wildlife recordings. (ii) Benchmarking: a frozen, checksummed hidden test set for RGB-only distance estimation and RGB+sparse-LiDAR depth completion, evaluated separately on animal and background pixels. (iii) Motion and tracking: metric 3D trajectories obtained by lifting 2D tracks using LiDAR, radar, or monocular depth. Across 48,723 animal frames, object-level radar calibration improves monocular depth over scene-level LiDAR calibration without requiring LiDAR at inference (AbsRel 0.120 vs. 0.198), although animal-level LiDAR remains more accurate (0.052). Across 431 tracks, once-per-track radar scaling reduces 3D position error four-fold (2.20 to 0.58 m) and distance-sampling detection-function bias from -13% to -2%, matching once-per-track LiDAR calibration. Transferring radar Doppler onto LiDAR produces motion masks 3.7 times denser than radar alone at 72% precision, reduces moving-animal LiDAR accumulation error by 37%, and estimates radial speed (r = 0.60), albeit with approximately 50% underestimation.
  </details>

- **[Where Do Long Captions Fail? Position-Aware Diagnosis and Reinforcement Learning for Detailed Image Captioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31111)**  `arXiv:2610.31111`  
  _Yuanze Hu, Zhichao Yang, Junwei Jing, Xin Yang, Wenxuan Liu, Tinghai Zhang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Detailed image captioning is usually framed as an amount problem: a model should mention more visual facts while avoiding hallucination. However, autoregressive VLMs generate captions as ordered sequences, and later generations may be produced under weaker or noisier visual grounding. We therefore argue that long-form captioning should be studied not only by what facts are mentioned, but also by where they appear. We introduce a position-aware rubric representation that decomposes dense human references into atomic visual claims, verifies generated captions against those claims, and induces a quartile-wise composition of supported, hallucinated, and generic content. This lens reveals two qualitatively different failures in long captions: models tend to produce unsupported specificity in the middle, while the final portion often escapes into vague, non-evidential language rather than simply accumulating more hallucination. A same-reference human control does not exhibit these patterns; additional human audits examine the visual validity of the induced labels. Motivated by this diagnosis, we propose PAGER (Position-Aware Grounded Evidence Reinforcement), a GRPO training objective with separate signals for supported fine-grained middle evidence, evidence-bearing tail continuation, claim coverage, and content-aware length control. The result is a closed loop: the same ordered claim representation identifies where long captions fail and supplies the position-specific reward needed to repair those failures. Experiments on detailed-caption benchmarks show that PAGER improves position-wise grounding and pairwise caption preference while preserving competitive global caption quality.
  </details>

- **[DRAFT: Decoding and Reconstructing 3D Room Layouts from fMRI and Camera Trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31108)**  `arXiv:2610.31108`  
  _Chi-Wei Lee, An-Kai Chen, Cheng-Han Chiang, Yi-Chun Chang, Po-Chih Kuo_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  While functional magnetic resonance imaging (fMRI) decoders reconstruct images and 3D objects, recovering the metric geometry of rooms from a moving viewpoint remains an open challenge. We introduce, to our knowledge for the first time, the task of reconstructing 3D layouts of held-out scenes using only the known camera trajectories and the fMRI signals of participants watching indoor walkthroughs, without any visual frames. The task is hard because of the temporal mismatch between rapid visual changes and blurred fMRI signals. Our method, DRAFT (Decoding and Reconstructing 3D room lAyouts from fMRI and Trajectories), extracts a compact layout representation from the early visual cortex and fuses this neural code with the geometric free space derived from camera motion. To evaluate it, we propose layout r, the Pearson correlation between predicted and ray-cast log depths. On ten held-out scenes (20 participants), DRAFT reaches a layout r of 0.480, outperforming the camera path alone (0.446) and seven fMRI decoders (at most 0.429). DRAFT also works as a plug-in that improves all seven decoders without retraining, raising their layout r from 0.171-0.429 to 0.487-0.493. Brain activity and camera motion can thus recover the metric layout of unseen rooms, a first step toward reconstructing the 3D spaces people see.
  </details>

- **[I$^2$-KAN: Interpretable Intra- and Inter-Modal Multi-Pathway Representation Learning for Multimodal Sentiment Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31087)**  `arXiv:2610.31087`  
  _Xiaoting Xie, Feng Qiao, Xianwei Zhang, Hailong Chen, Kaiwen Zheng, Joemon M. Jose, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal sentiment analysis (MSA) aims to infer sentiment by integrating linguistic, acoustic, and visual information. However, many fusion methods integrate modality-specific representations and cross-modal interactions into joint representations, making it difficult to determine whether predictive utility arises from a modality itself or from its interactions with others. More fundamentally, a modality's standalone predictive utility may not faithfully reflect its interaction-conditioned predictive utility. To address this issue, we propose I$^2$-KAN, an interpretable multi-pathway framework that explicitly maintains intra-modal and pairwise inter-modal information in separate structural pathways. Each pathway is refined by a Task-Decoupled Linear-Gated Denoising Bottleneck (TDL-GDB) to learn compact and task-relevant representations, followed by structured Kolmogorov-Arnold Network fusion that retains explicit pathway associations and supports multi-level interpretation. Extensive experiments on CMU-MOSI, CMU-MOSEI, and CH-SIMS v2 demonstrate strong overall performance. Ablation and interpretability analyses consistently show across the three benchmarks that low standalone predictive utility does not necessarily imply low interaction-conditioned predictive utility: a modality that appears weak in isolation can remain informative through pairwise interactions. These findings highlight the interaction-dependent nature of multimodal utility and suggest that modality utility should be characterized from both standalone and interaction-conditioned perspectives.
  </details>

- **[A General-Purpose Dual-Path Interaction Framework for Multi-View Medical Image Segmentation: Alleviating Data Volume Dependency in Small-Sample Regimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31065)**  `arXiv:2610.31065`  
  _Shuhuan Wang, Yuanyuan Cui, Pengju Wang, Huimin Guo, Ling Yue, Yin Gu, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The performance of medical image segmentation depends on a large amount of annotated data, and most segmentation models experience a significant drop in performance in low-sample-size scenarios. Existing semi-supervised and weakly supervised methods rely on specific network architectures, while traditional multi-view-assisted segmentation methods require strictly paired samples, which limits their clinical applications. To address these issues, we propose DPIF, a general-purpose plug-and-play dual-path interaction framework compatible with arbitrary encoder-decoder segmentation networks, which leverages cross-view anatomical complementarity to reduce annotation reliance. DPIF first performs Sinkhorn optimal transport-based cross-view distribution alignment to establish global semantic correspondence without sample-level pairing. It then adopts conditional mutual information-guided feature decoupling to enable bidirectional knowledge transfer only within the shared semantic space, suppressing view-specific noise. Finally, persistent homology-based topological regularization encodes anatomical invariants as unsupervised priors to mitigate overfitting. Experiments were conducted using 24 baseline models on a private multicenter echocardiography dataset and two public datasets. The results show that DPIF-enhanced models outperform single-view baseline models when trained on comparable amounts of data. Notably, most dual-path variants trained on 40\% - 60\% of the data achieve performance comparable to that of single-view models trained on the full dataset. This demonstrates that DPIF provides a general-purpose and scalable solution for data-scarce multi-view medical image segmentation problems.
  </details>

- **[ResGVC: Residual Coding for High-fidelity Generative Video Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31039)**  `arXiv:2610.31039`  
  _Ding Ding, Daowen Li, Ying Chen, Kai Li_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Generative video compression (GVC) methods usually employ decoder-side diffusion models to denoise reconstructed signals based on generative priors. Besides, most of these methods perform denoising in latent space rather than directly in pixel domain. In this paper, we propose ResGVC, a residual coding framework for GVC that guides denoising toward original pictures by transmitting residual signals. ResGVC first encodes the original pictures within a learned video codec, and then decomposes the distortion between the codec reconstruction and the original pictures into a sequence of residual segments, where each residual segment is acquired at each denoising step. Residual segments can further be compressed by a novel residual codec with a diffusion transformer as an entropy model. Unlike prior methods, ResGVC introduces explicit source constraints throughout denoising rather than relying on decoder-side generation alone. The diffusion-based entropy model also enables more effective distribution modeling capability based on the powerful generative priors. Moreover, denoising directly in pixel space avoids the irreversible information loss caused by latent representation bottlenecks. Experimental results show that, compared with the previous state-of-the-art method, ResGVC achieves 63.7% and 83.8% BD-rate reductions in terms of LPIPS and PSNR, respectively, while also delivering superior visual quality.
  </details>

- **[Diagnostic Loss: Asymmetric Supervision for Radiology Report Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30980)**  `arXiv:2610.30980`  
  _Ignacy Kolton, Wenxuan Li, Zongwei Zhou, Jarosław B. Ćwikła, Arkadiusz Sitek, Pedro R. A. S. Bassi, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Automated radiology report generation requires models to do more than produce fluent clinical narratives: they must explicitly state supported diagnoses while avoiding unsupported ones. However, existing medical vision-language models (MedVLMs) are typically trained with token-level maximum likelihood objectives, which prioritize text agreement over diagnostically critical terms. As a result, models can miss key diagnoses and introduce wrong ones. We identify this as an objective misalignment between conventional language modeling and the asymmetric diagnostic requirements of radiology reporting. To encode these requirements, we introduce Diagnostic Loss (DLoss), a differentiable, architecture-agnostic objective that operates directly on decoder token probabilities. DLoss separately aggregates probability mass over vocabularies associated with diagnoses supported by the reference report and with unsupported alternatives. Using asymmetric pooling across token positions in the report, it encourages supported diagnoses to receive sufficient probability in at least one position while suppressing spurious probability spikes for unsupported diagnoses across all positions during training. The loss can be applied to existing 3D MedVLMs without modifying their vision backbones, language decoders, or inference procedures, and introduces no additional inference-time computation. We train and evaluate DLoss on 50,000 abdominal CT scans across several 3D MedVLM architectures on both in-distribution and out-of-distribution report-generation benchmarks. Across these settings, DLoss improves diagnostic faithfulness while maintaining competitive performance on conventional language-generation metrics, showing the value of asymmetric sequence-level supervision for radiology report generation. The code for DLoss is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[GraphCAD: Geometry-Aware Diffusion on Edge-Adjacency Graphs for CAD Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30974)**  `arXiv:2610.30974`  
  _Wenhao Zheng, Xianggen Liu, Li Yue, Jiancheng Shi, Jiancheng Lv_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Generative modeling for Computer-Aided Design (CAD) remains fundamentally challenged by the need to capture the intricate interplay between discrete topological connectivity and precise spatial geometry inherent in Boundary Representations (B-reps). Existing generative approaches largely formulate CAD construction as a sequence modeling problem, but consequently lack an explicit topological inductive bias. In this work, we present GraphCAD, a geometry-aware generative framework that centers modeling on the Edge-Adjacency Graph (EAG), a novel graph-based CAD representation tailored for generative tasks. GraphCAD adopts a hybrid graph diffusion formulation to jointly model the discrete and continuous components of EAG and introduces a geometry-aware denoising mechanism that renders node attributes into spatial point sets. These point sets are encoded by an SE(3)-equivariant backbone and fused into the node representations, thereby grounding the generative process in physical geometry. To support this study, we construct EAGBench, a large-scale dataset of engineering CAD models. Extensive experiments show that GraphCAD can generate complex CAD models with more than 50 faces and achieves state-of-the-art performance in geometric fidelity and distributional alignment compared with sequence-based baselines.
  </details>

- **[Attention-guided classification and tumor localization of combined hepatocellular-cholangiocarcinoma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30944)**  `arXiv:2610.30944`  
  _Xiaowen Liang, Hélène Cazier, Aurélie Beaufrère, Miguel Albuquerque, Valérie Paradis, Cédric Wemmert_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Combined hepatocellular-cholangiocarcinomas (cHCC-CCA) are a type of cancer that share components of two other primary liver cancers: hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) and cholangiocarcinoma (CCA). Accurate diagnosis of cHCC-CCA, of which the behavior may resemble that of either HCC or CCA, remains a challenge due to strong intratumoral heterogeneity. Since the prognoses differ between liver cancer types, it is necessary to improve their morphological characterization and recognition in order to accurately identify cHCC-CCA. In this work, we use whole slide images (WSIs) of the two main types of liver cancer (HCC and CCA) and cHCC-CCA, stained with Hematoxylin-Eosin-Safran (HES), to train a classification model that can distinguish between these subtypes and provides a potential solution for localizing different types of tumors in the combined liver cancer images.
  </details>

- **[ICGF-CXRMate: Image-Conditioned Clinical Context Compression and Gated Fusion for Chest X-ray Report Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30928)**  `arXiv:2610.30928`  
  _Vu Minh Tran, Thuc Huynh, Son Thanh Luu, Doanh C. Bui_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Auxiliary patient data can improve chest X-ray report generation, but directly concatenating heterogeneous clinical information with visual tokens produces long, variable-length decoder prompts and may expose the model to redundant or image-irrelevant context. We propose ICGF-CXRMate, an image-conditioned clinical context compression and gated fusion framework. Learnable queries are first conditioned on the current chest X-ray representations and then attend to the patient-data sequence, compressing it into a fixed number of clinical context tokens. Gated cross-attention selectively integrates these tokens into the visual stream, while the compressed tokens are retained in the decoder prompt as a direct clinical pathway. Experiments on linked MIMIC-CXR and MIMIC-IV-ED examinations show that ICGF-CXRMate improves report quality while reducing the average prompt length. In Stage~2, it improves RadGraph-F1 from 24.00 to 28.86, CXR-BERT similarity from 56.86 to 62.80, and BLEU-4 from 5.14 to 7.96 compared with CXRMate-ED, while reducing the average prompt length from 373.89 to 288.37 tokens, corresponding to a 22.9\% reduction. After self-critical sequence training, ICGF-CXRMate outperforms CXRMate-ED on six of eight report-quality metrics in Stage~3. Ablation studies confirm the complementary contributions of image-conditioned compression, gated fusion, and direct retention of the compressed clinical tokens.
  </details>

- **[Making MLLM Latents Matter: Factor-Aware Predictive Learning for Cinematic Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30926)**  `arXiv:2610.30926`  
  _Songlin Yang, Zhe Wang, Xiaotong Zhao, Xi Wang, Ran Zhang, Shuai Li, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Cinematic video generation requires both temporal inference from limited inputs (i.e., sparse rather than frame-by-frame guidance) and factor-aware control of character, scene, action, and camera. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) offer useful priors for both capabilities, but their representations are not automatically actionable for video generation: a Video Diffusion Transformer (DiT) may bypass them through native conditioning pathways, and using them does not ensure either factor-aware control or generation-compatible temporal prediction. We propose Factor-Aware Predictive Learning (FAPL), which establishes effective MLLM latent use as a shared prerequisite and then targets the two capabilities with complementary learning signals. First, latent-only generator grounding trains the DiT with a frozen MLLM and connector, strengthening the DiT's use of MLLM latents for generation. Second, for factor-aware control, paired videos specify a target-factor change and non-target preservation, and factor-span replacement trains the DiT to realize this relationship in its output.
